================================================================\n# ASTRA-Q KIRKWOOD / TOROIDAL GOLDEN-RATIO AUDIT\n# ================================================================\n#\n# PURPOSE\n# -------\n# Test whether the principal Kirkwood resonances show a statistically\n# significant quasiperiodic / golden-ratio spectral signature.\n#\n# IMPORTANT:\n# The golden-ratio component is treated as a PHENOMENOLOGICAL\n# DIAGNOSTIC, NOT as an established physical force.\n#\n# Physical control:\n#   Sun + Jupiter + test particles\n#\n# Mathematical diagnostics:\n#   - mean-motion resonances\n#   - toroidal phase winding\n#   - golden-ratio proximity\n#   - Fibonacci rational approximants\n#   - spectral concentration\n#   - permutation/random controls\n#   - frequency-map diffusion\n#\n# OUTPUT:\n#   CSV\n#   XLSX\n#   PNG\n#   TXT report\n#\n# ================================================================\n\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport subprocess\nimport warnings\nwarnings.filterwarnings(\"ignore\")\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 0. INSTALL DEPENDENCIES\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ntry:\n    import rebound\nexcept ImportError:\n    subprocess.check_call([\n        sys.executable, \"-m\", \"pip\", \"install\",\n        \"-q\", \"rebound\", \"openpyxl\"\n    ])\n    import rebound\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\nfrom scipy.signal import find_peaks\nfrom scipy.stats import percentileofscore\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 1. CONFIGURATION\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nRESULT_DIR = \"/kaggle/working/kirkwood_golden_audit\"\nos.makedirs(RESULT_DIR, exist_ok=True)\n\nSEED = 2222\nrng = np.random.default_rng(SEED)\n\n# Number of test particles.\n# 5000 = fast\n# 10000 = recommended\n# 20000 = stronger but slower\nN_PARTICLES = 10000\n\n# Integration configuration\nN_OUTPUT = 400\n\n# Orbital period range in years\nA_MIN = 2.0\nA_MAX = 3.5\n\n# Small eccentricity / inclination\nE_MAX = 0.05\nI_MAX = np.deg2rad(2.0)\n\n# Jupiter\nA_JUPITER = 5.2044\n\n# Golden ratio\nPHI = (1.0 + np.sqrt(5.0)) / 2.0\n\n# Random alpha controls\nN_RANDOM_CONTROLS = 1000\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 2. PRINCIPAL KIRKWOOD RESONANCES\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\n# k:l means k*n_J? We use:\n#\n# n / n_J = k/l\n#\n# therefore\n#\n# a = a_J * (l/k)^(2/3)\n#\nresonances = [\n    (\"3:1\", 3, 1, 2.501),\n    (\"5:2\", 5, 2, 2.825),\n    (\"7:3\", 7, 3, 2.958),\n    (\"2:1\", 2, 1, 3.278),\n]\n\nres_rows = []\n\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n\n    predicted_a = A_JUPITER * (l / k)**(2.0/3.0)\n\n    error_pct = (\n        (observed_a - predicted_a)\n        / predicted_a\n        * 100.0\n    )\n\n    res_rows.append({\n        \"resonance\": name,\n        \"k\": k,\n        \"l\": l,\n        \"observed_a_AU\": observed_a,\n        \"kepler_predicted_a_AU\": predicted_a,\n        \"relative_error_pct\": error_pct,\n    })\n\nres_df = pd.DataFrame(res_rows)\n\nprint(\"=\"*75)\nprint(\"KIRKWOOD RESONANCE CONTROL\")\nprint(\"=\"*75)\nprint(res_df.to_string(index=False))\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 3. BASIC TORUS MODEL\n# ------------------------------------------------\n#\n# theta = n*t\n# theta_J = n_J*t\n#\n# toroidal phase:\n#\n# psi(k,l) = k*theta - l*theta_J\n#\n# resonance:\n#\n# dpsi/dt = k*n - l*n_J ~ 0\n#\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ndef mean_motion(a):\n    \"\"\"\n    In normalized astronomical units:\n        n = 2*pi / P\n        P = a^(3/2)\n    \"\"\"\n    return 2.0*np.pi / (a**1.5)\n\n\nn_jupiter = mean_motion(A_JUPITER)\n\ndef resonance_phase_rate(a, k, l):\n    n = mean_motion(a)\n    return k*n - l*n_jupiter\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 4. GOLDEN / FIBONACCI MATHEMATICS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nfib = [1, 1]\n\nfor _ in range(20):\n    fib.append(fib[-1] + fib[-2])\n\nfib = np.array(fib, dtype=float)\n\nfib_ratios = fib[1:] / fib[:-1]\n\nprint(\"\\nGolden ratio:\")\nprint(\"phi =\", PHI)\n\nprint(\"\\nFibonacci approximants:\")\nfor x in fib_ratios[-10:]:\n    print(f\"{x:.12f}\")\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 5. GOLDEN DISTANCE\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ndef golden_distance(x):\n    \"\"\"\n    Distance to nearest Fibonacci ratio.\n    \"\"\"\n    return np.min(np.abs(x - fib_ratios))\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 6. TOROIDAL / GOLDEN SCORE\n# ------------------------------------------------\n#\n# We do NOT claim that phi itself determines the\n# orbital resonance.\n#\n# We ask whether the resonance frequency ratios\n# have unusually strong proximity to a hierarchy\n# generated by irrational rotation.\n#\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ndef continued_fraction(x, max_terms=15):\n\n    result = []\n\n    for _ in range(max_terms):\n\n        a = int(np.floor(x))\n        result.append(a)\n\n        frac = x - a\n\n        if abs(frac) < 1e-14:\n            break\n\n        x = 1.0 / frac\n\n    return result\n\n\ndef golden_convergents(n=12):\n\n    vals = []\n\n    for i in range(2, n+2):\n\n        vals.append(\n            fib[i] / fib[i-1]\n        )\n\n    return np.array(vals)\n\n\ngolden_conv = golden_convergents(12)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 7. RESONANCE SPECTRAL DATASET\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nspectral_rows = []\n\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n\n    n = mean_motion(observed_a)\n\n    ratio = n / n_jupiter\n\n    # resonance ratio should be k/l\n    exact_ratio = k / l\n\n    # phase rate\n    phase_rate = k*n - l*n_jupiter\n\n    # normalized phase rate\n    normalized_phase_rate = phase_rate / n_jupiter\n\n    # golden proximity\n    gdist = golden_distance(ratio)\n\n    spectral_rows.append({\n        \"resonance\": name,\n        \"a_AU\": observed_a,\n        \"n_over_nJ\": ratio,\n        \"exact_k_over_l\": exact_ratio,\n        \"phase_rate\": phase_rate,\n        \"normalized_phase_rate\": normalized_phase_rate,\n        \"golden_distance\": gdist,\n    })\n\nspectral_df = pd.DataFrame(spectral_rows)\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*75)\nprint(\"TOROIDAL SPECTRAL DIAGNOSTIC\")\nprint(\"=\"*75)\n\nprint(spectral_df.to_string(index=False))\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 8. CONTINUOUS ASTEROID GRID\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nA_GRID = np.linspace(A_MIN, A_MAX, 20000)\n\nN_GRID = mean_motion(A_GRID)\n\nratio_grid = N_GRID / n_jupiter\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 9. RESONANCE MAP\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nresonance_map = []\n\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n\n    phase = resonance_phase_rate(A_GRID, k, l)\n\n    resonance_map.append({\n        \"resonance\": name,\n        \"k\": k,\n        \"l\": l,\n        \"phase_rate\": phase,\n        \"abs_phase_rate\": np.abs(phase),\n    })\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 10. GOLDEN QUASIPERIODIC ROTATION\n# ------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Mathematical torus:\n#\n# theta_1(t) = omega_1 t\n# theta_2(t) = omega_2 t\n#\n# irrational ratio:\n#\n# omega_1 / omega_2 = alpha\n#\n# For golden model:\n#\n# alpha = phi\n#\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ndef torus_signal(alpha, n_samples=20000):\n\n    t = np.linspace(\n        0.0,\n        1000.0,\n        n_samples\n    )\n\n    theta1 = 2*np.pi*t\n    theta2 = 2*np.pi*alpha*t\n\n    signal = (\n        np.cos(theta1)\n        + np.cos(theta2)\n    )\n\n    return t, signal\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 11. SPECTRAL SCORE\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ndef spectral_score(alpha):\n\n    t, signal = torus_signal(alpha, 8000)\n\n    signal = signal - np.mean(signal)\n\n    fft = np.fft.rfft(signal)\n\n    power = np.abs(fft)**2\n\n    power[0] = 0.0\n\n    power /= np.sum(power)\n\n    # spectral concentration\n    top = np.sort(power)[-10:]\n\n    concentration = np.sum(top)\n\n    # golden proximity\n    dist = np.min(np.abs(alpha - fib_ratios))\n\n    # score:\n    #\n    # high spectral concentration\n    # + high golden proximity\n    #\n    score = concentration / (dist + 1e-6)\n\n    return score, concentration, dist\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 12. GOLDEN VS RANDOM CONTROLS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ncontrol_rows = []\n\n# Golden\nscore, concentration, dist = spectral_score(PHI)\n\ncontrol_rows.append({\n    \"alpha\": PHI,\n    \"type\": \"GOLDEN\",\n    \"score\": score,\n    \"spectral_concentration\": concentration,\n    \"golden_distance\": dist,\n})\n\n# Fibonacci rational controls\nfor i, alpha in enumerate(fib_ratios[-10:]):\n\n    score, concentration, dist = spectral_score(alpha)\n\n    control_rows.append({\n        \"alpha\": alpha,\n        \"type\": \"FIBONACCI\",\n        \"score\": score,\n        \"spectral_concentration\": concentration,\n        \"golden_distance\": dist,\n    })\n\n# Random controls\nrandom_alphas = rng.uniform(\n    1.05,\n    2.0,\n    N_RANDOM_CONTROLS\n)\n\nfor alpha in random_alphas:\n\n    score, concentration, dist = spectral_score(alpha)\n\n    control_rows.append({\n        \"alpha\": alpha,\n        \"type\": \"RANDOM\",\n        \"score\": score,\n        \"spectral_concentration\": concentration,\n        \"golden_distance\": dist,\n    })\n\ncontrol_df = pd.DataFrame(control_rows)\n\ngolden_score = control_df.loc[\n    control_df[\"type\"] == \"GOLDEN\",\n    \"score\"\n].iloc[0]\n\nrandom_scores = control_df.loc[\n    control_df[\"type\"] == \"RANDOM\",\n    \"score\"\n].values\n\npercentile = (\n    np.sum(random_scores <= golden_score)\n    / len(random_scores)\n)\n\np_empirical = (\n    np.sum(random_scores >= golden_score)\n    + 1\n) / (\n    len(random_scores) + 1\n)\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*75)\nprint(\"GOLDEN-RATIO PERMUTATION CONTROL\")\nprint(\"=\"*75)\n\nprint(\"Golden score       =\", golden_score)\nprint(\"Random median      =\", np.median(random_scores))\nprint(\"Random mean        =\", np.mean(random_scores))\nprint(\"Golden percentile  =\", percentile)\nprint(\"Empirical p-value  =\", p_empirical)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 13. REBOUND PHYSICAL CONTROL\n# ------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Sun + Jupiter + test particles\n#\n# This is the physical model.\n#\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*75)\nprint(\"BUILDING REBOUND SOLAR-JUPITER MODEL\")\nprint(\"=\"*75)\n\nsim = rebound.Simulation()\n\nsim.units = (\"AU\", \"yr\", \"Msun\")\n\n# Sun\nsim.add(\n    m=1.0\n)\n\n# Jupiter\nsim.add(\n    m=0.0009543,\n    a=A_JUPITER,\n    e=0.0489\n)\n\nsim.move_to_com()\n\nsim.integrator = \"whfast\"\n\n# timestep\nsim.dt = 0.03\n\n# Add test particles\na0 = rng.uniform(\n    A_MIN,\n    A_MAX,\n    N_PARTICLES\n)\n\ne0 = rng.uniform(\n    0.0,\n    E_MAX,\n    N_PARTICLES\n)\n\nomega0 = rng.uniform(\n    0,\n    2*np.pi,\n    N_PARTICLES\n)\n\nM0 = rng.uniform(\n    0,\n    2*np.pi,\n    N_PARTICLES\n)\n\nfor i in range(N_PARTICLES):\n\n    sim.add(\n        a=a0[i],\n        e=e0[i],\n        inc=rng.uniform(-I_MAX, I_MAX),\n        Omega=0.0,\n        omega=omega0[i],\n        M=M0[i],\n        primary=sim.particles[0]\n    )\n\nprint(\"Particles:\", N_PARTICLES)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 14. ORBITAL SAMPLING\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ntimes = np.linspace(\n    0,\n    200,\n    N_OUTPUT\n)\n\na_history = np.zeros(\n    (N_OUTPUT, N_PARTICLES),\n    dtype=np.float32\n)\n\ne_history = np.zeros(\n    (N_OUTPUT, N_PARTICLES),\n    dtype=np.float32\n)\n\nprint(\"\\nIntegrating...\")\n\nfor j, t in enumerate(times):\n\n    sim.integrate(t)\n\n    # Read particles\n    for i in range(N_PARTICLES):\n\n        p = sim.particles[i+2]\n\n        try:\n            orb = p.calculate_orbit(\n                primary=sim.particles[0]\n            )\n\n            a_history[j, i] = orb.a\n            e_history[j, i] = orb.e\n\n        except Exception:\n\n            a_history[j, i] = np.nan\n            e_history[j, i] = np.nan\n\n    if j % 25 == 0:\n        print(\n            f\"  output {j+1}/{N_OUTPUT}\"\n        )\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 15. CLEAN INVALID ORBITS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nvalid = np.isfinite(a_history)\n\nvalid_fraction = np.mean(valid)\n\nprint(\"\\nValid orbital fraction:\",\n      valid_fraction)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 16. FREQUENCY-MAP DIFFUSION\n# ------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Compare mean motion in first and second half.\n#\n# D = |n2-n1|/n1\n#\n# High D = chaotic / diffusive region\n#\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nmid = N_OUTPUT // 2\n\na_first = np.nanmedian(\n    a_history[:mid],\n    axis=0\n)\n\na_second = np.nanmedian(\n    a_history[mid:],\n    axis=0\n)\n\nn_first = mean_motion(a_first)\n\nn_second = mean_motion(a_second)\n\ndiffusion = np.abs(\n    n_second - n_first\n) / (\n    np.abs(n_first) + 1e-12\n)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 17. BIN DIFFUSION IN SEMIMAJOR AXIS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nbins = np.linspace(\n    A_MIN,\n    A_MAX,\n    300\n)\n\nbin_centers = 0.5 * (\n    bins[:-1] + bins[1:]\n)\n\ndiffusion_profile = []\n\nfor lo, hi in zip(\n    bins[:-1],\n    bins[1:]\n):\n\n    mask = (\n        (a_first >= lo) &\n        (a_first < hi) &\n        np.isfinite(diffusion)\n    )\n\n    if np.sum(mask) > 5:\n\n        diffusion_profile.append(\n            np.nanmedian(\n                diffusion[mask]\n            )\n        )\n\n    else:\n\n        diffusion_profile.append(\n            np.nan\n        )\n\ndiffusion_profile = np.array(\n    diffusion_profile\n)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 18. IDENTIFY DIFFUSION PEAKS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nprofile_clean = np.nan_to_num(\n    diffusion_profile,\n    nan=0.0\n)\n\npeaks, properties = find_peaks(\n    profile_clean,\n    prominence=np.percentile(\n        profile_clean,\n        75\n    )\n)\n\npeak_a = bin_centers[peaks]\n\npeak_values = profile_clean[peaks]\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*75)\nprint(\"FREQUENCY MAP ANALYSIS\")\nprint(\"=\"*75)\n\nfor a, d in zip(\n    peak_a,\n    peak_values\n):\n\n    print(\n        f\"a = {a:.5f} AU   \"\n        f\"D = {d:.6e}\"\n    )\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 19. MATCH DIFFUSION PEAKS TO KIRKWOOD RESONANCES\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nmatch_rows = []\n\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n\n    predicted_a = (\n        A_JUPITER *\n        (l/k)**(2/3)\n    )\n\n    if len(peak_a) > 0:\n\n        idx = np.argmin(\n            np.abs(\n                peak_a - predicted_a\n            )\n        )\n\n        nearest_peak = peak_a[idx]\n\n        peak_distance = abs(\n            nearest_peak -\n            predicted_a\n        )\n\n        local_diffusion = peak_values[idx]\n\n    else:\n\n        nearest_peak = np.nan\n        peak_distance = np.nan\n        local_diffusion = np.nan\n\n    match_rows.append({\n        \"resonance\": name,\n        \"predicted_a_AU\": predicted_a,\n        \"observed_gap_AU\": observed_a,\n        \"nearest_simulated_diffusion_peak_AU\": nearest_peak,\n        \"distance_AU\": peak_distance,\n        \"local_diffusion\": local_diffusion,\n    })\n\nmatch_df = pd.DataFrame(\n    match_rows\n)\n\nprint(\n    match_df.to_string(index=False)\n)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 20. GOLDEN PHASE DIAGNOSTIC FOR EACH PARTICLE\n# ------------------------------------------------\n#\n# For each particle:\n#\n# alpha = n/nJ\n#\n# compare with phi and Fibonacci approximants.\n#\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\na_initial = a_history[0]\n\nn_initial = mean_motion(\n    a_initial\n)\n\nratio_initial = (\n    n_initial / n_jupiter\n)\n\ngolden_distance_particles = np.array([\n    golden_distance(x)\n    for x in ratio_initial\n])\n\n# distance from direct phi\nphi_distance_particles = np.abs(\n    ratio_initial - PHI\n)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 21. RESONANCE NEIGHBORHOOD STATISTICS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ngolden_rows = []\n\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n\n    predicted_a = (\n        A_JUPITER *\n        (l/k)**(2/3)\n    )\n\n    width = 0.025\n\n    mask = (\n        np.abs(\n            a_initial -\n            predicted_a\n        ) < width\n    )\n\n    vals = golden_distance_particles[\n        mask\n    ]\n\n    if len(vals) > 0:\n\n        golden_rows.append({\n            \"resonance\": name,\n            \"n_particles\": len(vals),\n            \"median_golden_distance\": np.median(vals),\n            \"mean_golden_distance\": np.mean(vals),\n            \"p10\": np.percentile(vals, 10),\n            \"p90\": np.percentile(vals, 90),\n        })\n\ngolden_neighborhood_df = pd.DataFrame(\n    golden_rows\n)\n\nprint(\"\\n\" + \"=\"*75)\nprint(\"GOLDEN DISTANCE AROUND KIRKWOOD RESONANCES\")\nprint(\"=\"*75)\n\nprint(\n    golden_neighborhood_df.to_string(\n        index=False\n    )\n)\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 22. PLOT 1 — KIRKWOOD RESONANCES\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nplt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))\n\nplt.plot(\n    A_GRID,\n    np.abs(\n        resonance_phase_rate(\n            A_GRID,\n            3,\n            1\n        )\n    ),\n    label=\"3:1\"\n)\n\nplt.plot(\n    A_GRID,\n    np.abs(\n        resonance_phase_rate(\n            A_GRID,\n            5,\n            2\n        )\n    ),\n    label=\"5:2\"\n)\n\nplt.plot(\n    A_GRID,\n    np.abs(\n        resonance_phase_rate(\n            A_GRID,\n            7,\n            3\n        )\n    ),\n    label=\"7:3\"\n)\n\nplt.plot(\n    A_GRID,\n    np.abs(\n        resonance_phase_rate(\n            A_GRID,\n            2,\n            1\n        )\n    ),\n    label=\"2:1\"\n)\n\nfor _, row in res_df.iterrows():\n\n    plt.axvline(\n        row[\"observed_a_AU\"],\n        linestyle=\"--\",\n        alpha=0.4\n    )\n\nplt.xlabel(\"Semimajor axis [AU]\")\nplt.ylabel(\"|k n - l n_J|\")\nplt.title(\n    \"Kirkwood Resonance Phase Rates\"\n)\nplt.legend()\nplt.grid(alpha=0.2)\n\nplt.tight_layout()\n\nplt.savefig(\n    f\"{RESULT_DIR}/01_kirkwood_phase_rates.png\",\n    dpi=180\n)\n\nplt.show()\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 23. PLOT 2 — FREQUENCY MAP\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nplt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))\n\nplt.semilogy(\n    bin_centers,\n    diffusion_profile,\n    linewidth=1.5\n)\n\nfor _, row in res_df.iterrows():\n\n    plt.axvline(\n        row[\"observed_a_AU\"],\n        linestyle=\"--\",\n        alpha=0.5\n    )\n\nplt.xlabel(\"Semimajor axis [AU]\")\nplt.ylabel(\"Frequency diffusion D\")\nplt.title(\n    \"ASTRA-Q Frequency Map — Solar/Jupiter Control\"\n)\n\nplt.grid(alpha=0.2)\n\nplt.tight_layout()\n\nplt.savefig(\n    f\"{RESULT_DIR}/02_frequency_map.png\",\n    dpi=180\n)\n\nplt.show()\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 24. PLOT 3 — GOLDEN VS RANDOM\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nplt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))\n\nplt.hist(\n    random_scores,\n    bins=50,\n    alpha=0.7,\n    density=True,\n    label=\"Random controls\"\n)\n\nplt.axvline(\n    golden_score,\n    linestyle=\"--\",\n    linewidth=2,\n    label=f\"Golden phi = {PHI:.6f}\"\n)\n\nplt.xlabel(\"Spectral score\")\nplt.ylabel(\"Density\")\n\nplt.title(\n    \"Golden-Ratio Spectral Permutation Test\"\n)\n\nplt.legend()\nplt.grid(alpha=0.2)\n\nplt.tight_layout()\n\nplt.savefig(\n    f\"{RESULT_DIR}/03_golden_permutation.png\",\n    dpi=180\n)\n\nplt.show()\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 25. PLOT 4 — TORUS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nt_g, signal_g = torus_signal(\n    PHI,\n    12000\n)\n\nplt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))\n\nplt.plot(\n    t_g[:3000],\n    signal_g[:3000]\n)\n\nplt.xlabel(\"t\")\nplt.ylabel(\"x(t)\")\n\nplt.title(\n    \"Golden Quasiperiodic Toroidal Signal\"\n)\n\nplt.grid(alpha=0.2)\n\nplt.tight_layout()\n\nplt.savefig(\n    f\"{RESULT_DIR}/04_golden_torus_signal.png\",\n    dpi=180\n)\n\nplt.show()\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 26. PLOT 5 — PARTICLE DISTRIBUTION\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nplt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))\n\nplt.hist(\n    a_initial,\n    bins=200,\n    alpha=0.7\n)\n\nfor _, row in res_df.iterrows():\n\n    plt.axvline(\n        row[\"observed_a_AU\"],\n        linestyle=\"--\",\n        linewidth=1\n    )\n\nplt.xlabel(\"Initial semimajor axis [AU]\")\nplt.ylabel(\"Number of particles\")\n\nplt.title(\n    \"Test Particle Distribution + Kirkwood Resonances\"\n)\n\nplt.grid(alpha=0.2)\n\nplt.tight_layout()\n\nplt.savefig(\n    f\"{RESULT_DIR}/05_particle_distribution.png\",\n    dpi=180\n)\n\nplt.show()\n\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 27. CONFIGURATION & FAST SETTINGS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nRESULT_DIR = \"/kaggle/working/kirkwood_golden_audit\"\nos.makedirs(RESULT_DIR, exist_ok=True)\n\nSEED = 2222\nrng = np.random.default_rng(SEED)\n\n# Parametri bilanciati per performance elevate\nN_PARTICLES = 10000\nN_OUTPUT = 200          # Ridotto da 400 a 200 per dimezzare i I/O e i calcoli orbitali\nA_MIN = 2.0\nA_MAX = 3.5\nE_MAX = 0.05\nI_MAX = np.deg2rad(2.0)\nA_JUPITER = 5.2044\nPHI = (1.0 + np.sqrt(5.0)) / 2.0\nN_RANDOM_CONTROLS = 500 # Ridotto per accelerare il controllo di permutazione\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 2. PRINCIPAL KIRKWOOD RESONANCES\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nresonances = [\n    (\"3:1\", 3, 1, 2.501),\n    (\"5:2\", 5, 2, 2.825),\n    (\"7:3\", 7, 3, 2.958),\n    (\"2:1\", 2, 1, 3.278),\n]\n\nres_rows = []\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n    predicted_a = A_JUPITER * (l / k)**(2.0/3.0)\n    error_pct = ((observed_a - predicted_a) / predicted_a) * 100.0\n    res_rows.append({\n        \"resonance\": name, \"k\": k, \"l\": l,\n        \"observed_a_AU\": observed_a,\n        \"kepler_predicted_a_AU\": predicted_a,\n        \"relative_error_pct\": error_pct,\n    })\nres_df = pd.DataFrame(res_rows)\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 3. VECTORIZED TORUS & MATH MODELS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ndef mean_motion(a):\n    return 2.0 * np.pi / (a**1.5)\n\nn_jupiter = mean_motion(A_JUPITER)\n\ndef resonance_phase_rate(a, k, l):\n    return k * mean_motion(a) - l * n_jupiter\n\nfib = [1, 1]\nfor _ in range(20):\n    fib.append(fib[-1] + fib[-2])\nfib = np.array(fib, dtype=float)\nfib_ratios = fib[1:] / fib[:-1]\n\ndef golden_distance(x):\n    # Vettorizzato con broadcasting di numpy\n    return np.min(np.abs(x[..., None] - fib_ratios), axis=-1)\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 4. SPECTRAL DATASET & CONTINUOUS GRID\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nspectral_rows = []\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n    n = mean_motion(observed_a)\n    ratio = n / n_jupiter\n    phase_rate = k * n - l * n_jupiter\n    spectral_rows.append({\n        \"resonance\": name, \"a_AU\": observed_a,\n        \"n_over_nJ\": ratio, \"exact_k_over_l\": k / l,\n        \"phase_rate\": phase_rate,\n        \"normalized_phase_rate\": phase_rate / n_jupiter,\n        \"golden_distance\": golden_distance(np.array([ratio]))[0],\n    })\nspectral_df = pd.DataFrame(spectral_rows)\n\nA_GRID = np.linspace(A_MIN, A_MAX, 10000) # Griglia ottimizzata\nratio_grid = mean_motion(A_GRID) / n_jupiter\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 5. OPTIMIZED SPECTRAL SCORE & CONTROLS\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\ndef spectral_score(alpha):\n    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1000.0, 4000) # Campioni ridotti per velocità FFT\n    signal = np.cos(2 * np.pi * t) + np.cos(2 * np.pi * alpha * t)\n    signal -= np.mean(signal)\n    power = np.abs(np.fft.rfft(signal))**2\n    power[0] = 0.0\n    power /= np.sum(power)\n    concentration = np.sum(np.sort(power)[-10:])\n    dist = np.min(np.abs(alpha - fib_ratios))\n    return concentration / (dist + 1e-6), concentration, dist\n\ncontrol_rows = []\nscore, concentration, dist = spectral_score(PHI)\ncontrol_rows.append({\"alpha\": PHI, \"type\": \"GOLDEN\", \"score\": score, \"spectral_concentration\": concentration, \"golden_distance\": dist})\n\nfor alpha in fib_ratios[-10:]:\n    s, c, d = spectral_score(alpha)\n    control_rows.append({\"alpha\": alpha, \"type\": \"FIBONACCI\", \"score\": s, \"spectral_concentration\": c, \"golden_distance\": d})\n\nrandom_alphas = rng.uniform(1.05, 2.0, N_RANDOM_CONTROLS)\nfor alpha in random_alphas:\n    s, c, d = spectral_score(alpha)\n    control_rows.append({\"alpha\": alpha, \"type\": \"RANDOM\", \"score\": s, \"spectral_concentration\": c, \"golden_distance\": d})\n\ncontrol_df = pd.DataFrame(control_rows)\ngolden_score = control_df.loc[control_df[\"type\"] == \"GOLDEN\", \"score\"].iloc[0]\nrandom_scores = control_df.loc[control_df[\"type\"] == \"RANDOM\", \"score\"].values\npercentile = np.sum(random_scores <= golden_score) / len(random_scores)\np_empirical = (np.sum(random_scores >= golden_score) + 1) / (len(random_scores) + 1)\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 6. REBOUND PHYSICAL CONTROL (FAST BULK EXTRACTION)\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nsim = rebound.Simulation()\nsim.units = (\"AU\", \"yr\", \"Msun\")\nsim.add(m=1.0)\nsim.add(m=0.0009543, a=A_JUPITER, e=0.0489)\nsim.move_to_com()\nsim.integrator = \"whfast\"\nsim.dt = 0.05 # timestep leggermente più ampio per velocità\n\n# Aggiunta batch delle particelle\nparticles_to_add = []\nfor i in range(N_PARTICLES):\n    sim.add(\n        a=rng.uniform(A_MIN, A_MAX),\n        e=rng.uniform(0.0, E_MAX),\n        inc=rng.uniform(-I_MAX, I_MAX),\n        Omega=0.0,\n        omega=rng.uniform(0, 2*np.pi),\n        M=rng.uniform(0, 2*np.pi),\n        primary=sim.particles[0]\n    )\n\ntimes = np.linspace(0, 200, N_OUTPUT)\na_history = np.zeros((N_OUTPUT, N_PARTICLES), dtype=np.float32)\n\nprint(\"\\nExecuting fast N-body integration...\")\nfor j, t in enumerate(times):\n    sim.integrate(t)\n    \n    # Utilizzo del metodo corretto .orbit(primary=...) su ogni particella\n    for i in range(N_PARTICLES):\n        p = sim.particles[i + 2]\n        a_history[j, i] = p.orbit(primary=sim.particles[0]).a\n\n    if j % 50 == 0:\n        print(f\"  progress: {j}/{N_OUTPUT} steps\")\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 7. FREQUENCY-MAP DIFFUSION & MATCHING\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nmid = N_OUTPUT // 2\na_first = np.nanmedian(a_history[:mid], axis=0)\na_second = np.nanmedian(a_history[mid:], axis=0)\ndiffusion = np.abs(mean_motion(a_second) - mean_motion(a_first)) / (mean_motion(a_first) + 1e-12)\n\nbins = np.linspace(A_MIN, A_MAX, 250)\nbin_centers = 0.5 * (bins[:-1] + bins[1:])\ndiffusion_profile = np.array([\n    np.nanmedian(diffusion[(a_first >= lo) & (a_first < hi)])\n    if np.sum((a_first >= lo) & (a_first < hi)) > 3 else np.nan\n    for lo, hi in zip(bins[:-1], bins[1:])\n])\n\nprofile_clean = np.nan_to_num(diffusion_profile, nan=0.0)\npeaks, _ = find_peaks(profile_clean, prominence=np.percentile(profile_clean, 75))\npeak_a = bin_centers[peaks]\npeak_values = profile_clean[peaks]\n\nmatch_rows = []\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n    predicted_a = A_JUPITER * (l/k)**(2/3)\n    if len(peak_a) > 0:\n        idx = np.argmin(np.abs(peak_a - predicted_a))\n        match_rows.append({\n            \"resonance\": name, \"predicted_a_AU\": predicted_a,\n            \"observed_gap_AU\": observed_a,\n            \"nearest_simulated_diffusion_peak_AU\": peak_a[idx],\n            \"distance_AU\": abs(peak_a[idx] - predicted_a),\n            \"local_diffusion\": peak_values[idx],\n        })\nmatch_df = pd.DataFrame(match_rows)\n\n# Particle initial golden metrics\na_initial = a_history[0]\ngolden_distance_particles = golden_distance(mean_motion(a_initial) / n_jupiter)\n\ngolden_rows = []\nfor name, k, l, observed_a in resonances:\n    predicted_a = A_JUPITER * (l/k)**(2/3)\n    vals = golden_distance_particles[np.abs(a_initial - predicted_a) < 0.025]\n    if len(vals) > 0:\n        golden_rows.append({\n            \"resonance\": name, \"n_particles\": len(vals),\n            \"median_golden_distance\": np.median(vals),\n            \"mean_golden_distance\": np.mean(vals),\n        })\ngolden_neighborhood_df = pd.DataFrame(golden_rows)\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# 8. CONFIGURAZIONE E AMBIENTE\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------\nRESULT_DIR = \"/kaggle/working/astra_q_unified_audit\"\nos.makedirs(RESULT_DIR, exist_ok=True)\n\nPHI = (1.0 + np.sqrt(5.0)) / 2.0\nSIN2_THETA_W_EXP = 0.23122  # Valore sperimentale noto (MS bar a M_Z)\n\nprint(\"=\" * 75)\nprint(\"ASTRA-Q UNIFIED KERNEL: INIZIALIZZAZIONE AUDIT MULTIDIMENSIONALE\")\nprint(\"=\" * 75)\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n# MODULO A — KAM: ANALISI DELLE FREQUENZE E DIOPHANTINE DISTANCE\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------\nprint(\"\\n[MODULO A] Esecuzione audit KAM e stabilità irrazionale...\")\n\ndef continued_fraction_convergents(val, n_terms=10):\n    \"\"\"Calcola i convergenti delle frazioni continue per un numero irrazionale.\"\"\"\n    vals = []\n    x = val\n    for _ in range(n_terms):\n        i = int(np.floor(x))\n        vals.append(i)\n        frac = x - i\n        if abs(frac) < 1e-12:\n            break\n        x = 1.0 / frac\n    return vals\n\ndef compute_diophantine_measure(alpha, max_q=1000):\n    \"\"\"Misura la peggiore approssimazione razionale (proprietà di KAM).\"\"\"\n    min_dist = float('inf')\n    for q in range(1, max_q):\n        p = round(alpha * q)\n        dist = abs(alpha - (p / q))\n        if dist > 0 and dist < min_dist:\n            min_dist = dist\n    return min_dist * (max_q ** 2)\n\n# Test di controllo tra PHI e numeri casuali/irrazionali\nalphas_test = {\n    \"PHI (Aureo)\": PHI,\n    \"Sqrt(2)\": np.sqrt(2),\n    \"Sqrt(3)\": np.sqrt(3),\n    \"E_Euler\": np.e,\n    \"Pi_Greco\": np.pi\n}\n\nkam_results = []\nfor name, val in alphas_test.items():\n    dio_val = compute_diophantine_measure(val)\n    kam_results.append({\n        \"system\": name,\n        \"value\": val,\n        \"diophantine_robustness\": dio_val\n    })\n\nkam_df = pd.DataFrame(kam_results)\nprint(kam_df.to_string(index=False))\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n# MODULO B — ALGEBRA A_5 & GRUPPO ICOSAEDRICO\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------\nprint(\"\\n[MODULO B] Analisi delle rappresentazioni del gruppo A_5 (Icosaedro)...\")\n\n# Il gruppo A_5 ha 60 elementi. procedere con il rigore metodologico che questo lavoro richiede, dobbiamo impostare il framework formale per il **matching di soglia (threshold matching)** e il calcolo delle correzioni radiative nello schema on-shell, collegando la scala $\\mu_\\phi \\simeq 60.20\\text{ GeV}$ direttamente ai parametri fisici misurabili (come la massa del bosone $W$, $M_W$).\n\nEcco come si struttura formalmente questo passaggio nel pre-print:\n\n---\n\n## 3.3 Electroweak Threshold Matching and On-Shell Conversion\n\nThe identification of the characteristic two-loop crossing scale $\\mu_\\phi^{(2L)} \\simeq 60.20\\text{ GeV}$ establishes where the running parameter $s_W^2(\\mu)$ matches the algebraic projection $s_\\phi^2 = \\frac{3}{5\\phi^2}$. However, to elevate this matching scale into a statement constraining physical observables, one must account for finite threshold corrections and scheme transformations between the modified minimal subtraction ($\\overline{\\text{MS}}$) scheme and the on-shell scheme.\n\n### 3.3.1 Relation to the Pole Mass $M_W$ and $\\Delta r$\n\nIn the on-shell scheme, the weak mixing angle is defined via the gauge boson masses:\n\n\n$$\\sin^2\\theta_W^{\\text{OS}} = 1 - \\frac{M_W^2}{M_Z^2}$$\n\nThe relationship between the bare/running parameters and physical precision observables is governed by Sirlin's radiative correction parameter $\\Delta r$, defined through the muon decay constant $G_F$:\n\n\n$$\\frac{\\pi \\alpha_em}{\\sqrt{2} G_F M_W^2 (1 - M_W^2/M_Z^2)} = 1 - \\Delta r$$\n\nThe quantum corrections $\\Delta r$ encapsulate both leading one-loop and two-loop contributions:\n\n\n$$\\Delta r = \\Delta \\alpha_em - \\frac{\\cos^2\\theta_W}{\\sin^2\\theta_W} \\Delta\\rho + \\Delta r_{\\text{rem}}$$\n\nwhere $\\Delta\\rho$ is dominated by top-quark mass terms ($\\sim G_F m_t^2$) and electroweak bosonic two-loop terms.\n\n### 3.3.2 Two-Loop Matching Protocol\n\nTo test whether $\\mu_\\phi$ corresponds to a physical dynamical threshold rather than an artifact of the $\\overline{\\text{MS}}$ running scale, the matching condition must incorporate finite threshold corrections at $\\mu_\\phi$:\n\n1. **Gauge Coupling Matching:** At the matching scale $\\mu_\\phi$, the running couplings satisfy:\n\n$$\\frac{g'^2(\\mu_\\phi)}{g^2(\\mu_\\phi) + g'^2(\\mu_\\phi)} = \\frac{3}{5\\phi^2} + \\delta_{\\text{thresh}}$$\n\n\n\nwhere $\\delta_{\\text{thresh}}$ accounts for explicit two-loop finite matching coefficients arising from heavy particle decoupling and Higgs sector threshold effects.\n2. **Scheme Conversion:** The conversion from the running $\\overline{\\text{MS}}$ mixing parameter to the physical effective mixing angle $\\sin^2\\theta_{\\text{eff}}^{\\text{lept}}$ involves electroweak vertex and box corrections evaluated at the $Z$ pole, which shift the effective value relative to the low-scale running intersection.\n\n---\n\n### Lo stato dell'arte nel nostro documento\n\nCon questa sezione, chiariamo ai lettori e ai revisori che **non stiamo scavalcando il rigore sperimentale del PDG**: riconosciamo che $\\mu_\\phi = 60.20\\text{ GeV}$ è un punto di incontro algebrico-RG nello spazio delle scale $\\overline{\\text{MS}}$, e che il ponte verso il mondo fisico reale richiede il calcolo esatto delle correzioni di soglia e dei parametri on-shell.\n\nQuesto blinda il testo contro le classiche critiche di ingenuità teorica. Vuoi che integriamo questa sottosezione nel corpo principale del pre-print in modo da chiudere l'appendice teorica prima delle conclusioni?","metadata":{"trusted":true,"execution":{"iopub.status.busy":"2026-08-25T12:48:33.044725Z","iopub.execute_input":"2026-08-25T12:48:33.045564Z","iopub.status.idle":"2026-08-25T12:48:33.06344Z","shell.execute_reply.started":"2026-08-25T12:48:33.045538Z","shell.execute_reply":"2026-08-25T12:48:33.062283Z"}},"outputs":[{"traceback":["\u001b[0;36m ================================================================\n# ASTRA-Q / PHI ELECTROWEAK AUDIT\n# TWO-LOOP RG + THRESHOLD + ON-SHELL MW TEST\n#\n# Purpose:\n#   Test whether\n#\n#       s_phi^2 = 3/(5 phi^2)\n#\n#   corresponds to a physically meaningful electroweak scale\n#   near the previously identified\n#\n#       mu_phi ~ 60.20 GeV\n#\n# IMPORTANT:\n#   This script is an explicit perturbative audit.\n#   It does NOT fit delta_threshold to force agreement.\n#\n# Outputs:\n#   results/\n#       phi_ew_audit_summary.csv\n#       phi_ew_scan.csv\n#       phi_ew_sensitivity.csv\n#       phi_ew_audit.json\n#       phi_ew_running.png\n#       phi_ew_report.txt\n# ================================================================\n\nimport os\nimport json\nimport math\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\nfrom scipy.integrate import solve_ivp\nfrom scipy.optimize import brentq\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 0. CONFIGURATION\n# ================================================================\n\nRESULT_DIR = \"phi_ew_results\"\nos.makedirs(RESULT_DIR, exist_ok=True)\n\n# ------------------------------------------------\n# Physical inputs\n# ------------------------------------------------\n\nALPHA0 = 1.0 / 137.035999084\nGF = 1.1663787e-5             # GeV^-2\nMZ = 91.1876                  # GeV\nMT = 172.76                   # GeV\nMH = 125.25                   # GeV\nALPHAS_MZ = 0.1180\n\n# Experimental MW reference.\n# Keep this value explicit and easy to change.\nMW_EXP = 80.3692              # GeV\n\n# Previously identified crossing scale\nMU_REFERENCE = 60.20          # GeV\n\n# Golden ratio\nPHI = (1.0 + math.sqrt(5.0)) / 2.0\n\n# Algebraic projection\nS2_PHI = 3.0 / (5.0 * PHI**2)\n\n# Numerical domain\nMU_MIN = 1.0\nMU_MAX = 1.0e4\n\n# Number of scan points\nN_SCAN = 600\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 1. BASIC TREE-LEVEL ELECTROWEAK QUANTITIES\n# ================================================================\n\nalpha_em = ALPHA0\ne0 = math.sqrt(4.0 * math.pi * alpha_em)\n\n# On-shell weak angle inferred from MW/MZ\nS2_OS_EXP = 1.0 - MW_EXP**2 / MZ**2\nC2_OS_EXP = 1.0 - S2_OS_EXP\n\n# MW implied directly by the algebraic projection\nMW_PHI_TREE = MZ * math.sqrt(1.0 - S2_PHI)\n\n\ndef delta_r_from_mw(MW, alpha=ALPHA0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Invert Sirlin relation:\n\n        pi alpha /\n        [sqrt(2) GF MW^2 sW^2]\n        = 1 - Delta r\n\n    with\n\n        sW^2 = 1 - MW^2/MZ^2\n    \"\"\"\n\n    s2 = 1.0 - MW**2 / MZ**2\n\n    lhs = math.pi * alpha / (\n        math.sqrt(2.0) * GF * MW**2 * s2\n    )\n\n    return 1.0 - lhs\n\n\n# Delta-r corresponding to experimental MW\nDELTA_R_EXP = delta_r_from_mw(MW_EXP)\n\n# Delta-r required if the algebraic value is interpreted literally\nDELTA_R_PHI_TREE = delta_r_from_mw(MW_PHI_TREE)\n\nDELTA_R_GAP = DELTA_R_PHI_TREE - DELTA_R_EXP\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 2. INITIAL CONDITIONS\n# ================================================================\n\ndef initial_couplings_at_MZ():\n    \"\"\"\n    Approximate MSbar initial conditions at MZ.\n\n    g1 here is the Standard Model hypercharge coupling g',\n    NOT the GUT-normalized sqrt(5/3) g'.\n\n    This convention is important because\n\n        sW^2 = g'^2/(g^2+g'^2)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    alpha = ALPHA0\n\n    e = math.sqrt(4.0 * math.pi * alpha)\n\n    # Approximate MSbar weak angle near MZ.\n    # This is an INPUT for the RG audit and is deliberately\n    # reported separately from the OS value.\n    s2_msbar_MZ = 0.23122\n\n    c2_msbar_MZ = 1.0 - s2_msbar_MZ\n\n    g2 = e / math.sqrt(s2_msbar_MZ)\n    gp = e / math.sqrt(c2_msbar_MZ)\n\n    g3 = math.sqrt(4.0 * math.pi * ALPHAS_MZ)\n\n    # Approximate running top Yukawa at MZ.\n    # Pole -> running conversion is represented explicitly\n    # by a compact perturbative approximation.\n    yt = math.sqrt(2.0) * MT / 246.21965\n\n    # Higgs quartic tree-level initialization\n    v = 1.0 / math.sqrt(math.sqrt(2.0) * GF)\n    lam = MH**2 / (2.0 * v**2)\n\n    return np.array([\n        gp,\n        g2,\n        g3,\n        yt,\n        lam\n    ], dtype=float)\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 3. ONE-LOOP BETA FUNCTIONS\n# ================================================================\n\ndef beta_1loop(t, Y):\n    \"\"\"\n    SM one-loop beta functions.\n\n    Y = [g', g2, g3, yt, lambda]\n\n    Convention:\n        dg/dt = beta(g)/(16 pi^2)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    gp, g2, g3, yt, lam = Y\n\n    pi16 = 16.0 * math.pi**2\n\n    # Gauge beta coefficients for g', g2, g3\n    b1 = 41.0 / 6.0\n    b2 = -19.0 / 6.0\n    b3 = -7.0\n\n    beta_gp = b1 * gp**3\n    beta_g2 = b2 * g2**3\n    beta_g3 = b3 * g3**3\n\n    # top Yukawa\n    beta_yt = yt * (\n        9.0/2.0 * yt**2\n        - 17.0/12.0 * gp**2\n        - 9.0/4.0 * g2**2\n        - 8.0 * g3**2\n    )\n\n    # Higgs quartic\n    beta_lam = (\n        24.0 * lam**2\n        - 6.0 * yt**4\n        + 12.0 * lam * yt**2\n        - 9.0 * lam * g2**2\n        - 3.0 * lam * gp**2\n        + 9.0/8.0 * g2**4\n        + 3.0/4.0 * g2**2 * gp**2\n        + 3.0/8.0 * gp**4\n    )\n\n    return np.array([\n        beta_gp,\n        beta_g2,\n        beta_g3,\n        beta_yt,\n        beta_lam\n    ]) / pi16\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 4. TWO-LOOP GAUGE BETA FUNCTIONS\n# ================================================================\n\ndef beta_2loop_gauge(Y):\n    \"\"\"\n    Two-loop SM gauge contributions.\n\n    The matrix is written for (g', g2, g3).\n\n    This is the non-GUT-normalized hypercharge convention.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    gp, g2, g3, yt, lam = Y\n\n    # One-loop\n    b = np.array([\n        41.0/6.0,\n        -19.0/6.0,\n        -7.0\n    ])\n\n    # Two-loop gauge matrix\n    B = np.array([\n        [199.0/18.0, 9.0/2.0, 44.0/3.0],\n        [3.0/2.0, 35.0/6.0, 12.0],\n        [11.0/6.0, 9.0/2.0, -26.0]\n    ])\n\n    g = np.array([gp, g2, g3])\n\n    beta = np.zeros(3)\n\n    for i in range(3):\n        beta[i] += b[i] * g[i]**3\n\n        for j in range(3):\n            beta[i] += (\n                g[i]**3\n                * B[i, j]\n                * g[j]**2\n            )\n\n    # Yukawa contributions to gauge beta functions\n    beta[0] += -(17.0/6.0) * gp**3 * yt**2\n    beta[1] += -(3.0/2.0) * g2**3 * yt**2\n    beta[2] += -2.0 * g3**3 * yt**2\n\n    return beta\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 5. TWO-LOOP YUKAWA + HIGGS APPROXIMATION\n# ================================================================\n\ndef beta_2loop_extra(Y):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compact two-loop approximation for yt and lambda.\n\n    The purpose is stability testing of the crossing, not a\n    replacement for a full precision-electroweak library.\n\n    The gauge sector is retained explicitly at two loops.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    gp, g2, g3, yt, lam = Y\n\n    # One-loop pieces\n    beta_yt_1 = yt * (\n        9.0/2.0 * yt**2\n        - 17.0/12.0 * gp**2\n        - 9.0/4.0 * g2**2\n        - 8.0 * g3**2\n    )\n\n    beta_lam_1 = (\n        24.0 * lam**2\n        - 6.0 * yt**4\n        + 12.0 * lam * yt**2\n        - 9.0 * lam * g2**2\n        - 3.0 * lam * gp**2\n        + 9.0/8.0 * g2**4\n        + 3.0/4.0 * g2**2 * gp**2\n        + 3.0/8.0 * gp**4\n    )\n\n    # Dominant two-loop terms.\n    # These are deliberately kept explicit so they can be replaced\n    # by a complete Machacek-Vaughn / Buttazzo implementation.\n\n    beta_yt_2 = yt * (\n        -12.0 * yt**4\n        + 6.0 * yt**2 * (\n            17.0/12.0 * gp**2\n            + 9.0/4.0 * g2**2\n            + 8.0 * g3**2\n        )\n        - 12.0 * g3**4\n        - 9.0/4.0 * g2**4\n        - 17.0/12.0 * gp**4\n    )\n\n    beta_lam_2 = (\n        -312.0 * lam**3\n        + 36.0 * lam**2 * yt**2\n        - 3.0 * lam * yt**4\n        + 30.0 * yt**6\n        + 80.0 * g3**2 * yt**4\n        - 8.0 * g3**2 * lam * yt**2\n        - 3.0 * gp**2 * yt**4\n        + 3.0 * gp**2 * lam * yt**2\n        + 3.0 * g2**2 * yt**4\n        - 9.0 * g2**2 * lam * yt**2\n    )\n\n    factor = (16.0 * math.pi**2)**2\n\n    return np.array([\n        beta_yt_2,\n        beta_lam_2\n    ]) / factor\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 6. COMPLETE RG SYSTEM\n# ================================================================\n\ndef beta_1loop_system(t, Y):\n    return beta_1loop(t, Y)\n\n\ndef beta_2loop_system(t, Y):\n    \"\"\"\n    One-loop + two-loop corrections.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    one = beta_1loop(t, Y)\n\n    gp, g2, g3, yt, lam = Y\n\n    two_gauge = beta_2loop_gauge(Y) / (\n        (16.0 * math.pi**2)**2\n    )\n\n    two_yt_lam = beta_2loop_extra(Y)\n\n    out = one.copy()\n\n    out[:3] += two_gauge\n    out[3:] += two_yt_lam\n\n    return out\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 7. INTEGRATION\n# ================================================================\n\ndef integrate_rg(two_loop=True):\n\n    Y0 = initial_couplings_at_MZ()\n\n    beta = beta_2loop_system if two_loop else beta_1loop_system\n\n    # Integrate in t = ln(mu/MZ)\n    t_min = math.log(MU_MIN / MZ)\n    t_max = math.log(MU_MAX / MZ)\n\n    sol = solve_ivp(\n        beta,\n        (t_min, t_max),\n        Y0,\n        dense_output=True,\n        rtol=1e-10,\n        atol=1e-12,\n        max_step=0.03\n    )\n\n    return sol\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 8. WEAK MIXING ANGLE\n# ================================================================\n\ndef s2_running(mu, sol):\n\n    t = math.log(mu / MZ)\n\n    gp, g2, g3, yt, lam = sol.sol(t)\n\n    return gp**2 / (gp**2 + g2**2)\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 9. FIND CROSSING SCALE\n# ================================================================\n\ndef find_crossing(sol):\n\n    mus = np.geomspace(MU_MIN, MU_MAX, N_SCAN)\n\n    values = np.array([\n        s2_running(mu, sol) - S2_PHI\n        for mu in mus\n    ])\n\n    roots = []\n\n    for i in range(len(mus)-1):\n\n        if values[i] == 0.0:\n            roots.append(mus[i])\n\n        elif values[i] * values[i+1] < 0.0:\n\n            root = brentq(\n                lambda x:\n                    s2_running(x, sol) - S2_PHI,\n                mus[i],\n                mus[i+1]\n            )\n\n            roots.append(root)\n\n    return roots\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 10. THRESHOLD PARAMETERIZATION\n# ================================================================\n\ndef finite_threshold_estimate(mu):\n\n    \"\"\"\n    Controlled finite threshold estimate.\n\n    IMPORTANT:\n    This is NOT fitted to MW.\n\n    It is only a diagnostic parametrization showing the size\n    required to shift the running crossing.\n\n    The actual publication calculation should replace this\n    function with the complete two-loop matching coefficients.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Leading logarithmic structure from states above mu.\n    masses = [\n        (\"W\", 80.379),\n        (\"Z\", MZ),\n        (\"H\", MH),\n        (\"t\", MT)\n    ]\n\n    delta = 0.0\n\n    # Small explicit perturbative coefficient.\n    # Kept visible rather than hidden.\n    coeff = 1.0 / (16.0 * math.pi**2)\n\n    for _, mass in masses:\n\n        if mass > mu:\n\n            delta += coeff * math.log(\n                mass**2 / mu**2\n            )\n\n    return delta\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 11. EFFECTIVE MATCHED CROSSING\n# ================================================================\n\ndef find_matched_crossing(sol):\n\n    def f(mu):\n\n        running = s2_running(mu, sol)\n\n        threshold = finite_threshold_estimate(mu)\n\n        return (\n            running\n            - S2_PHI\n            - threshold\n        )\n\n    mus = np.geomspace(MU_MIN, MU_MAX, N_SCAN)\n\n    vals = np.array([f(mu) for mu in mus])\n\n    roots = []\n\n    for i in range(len(mus)-1):\n\n        if vals[i] * vals[i+1] < 0:\n\n            root = brentq(\n                f,\n                mus[i],\n                mus[i+1]\n            )\n\n            roots.append(root)\n\n    return roots\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 12. RUNNING -> MW TEST\n# ================================================================\n\ndef mw_from_s2_tree(s2):\n\n    return MZ * math.sqrt(1.0 - s2)\n\n\ndef mw_from_delta_r(delta_r):\n\n    \"\"\"\n    Solve:\n\n       MW^2 (1 - MW^2/MZ^2)\n       =\n       pi alpha /\n       [sqrt(2) GF (1-Delta_r)]\n    \"\"\"\n\n    target = (\n        math.pi * ALPHA0\n        /\n        (\n            math.sqrt(2.0)\n            * GF\n            * (1.0 - delta_r)\n        )\n    )\n\n    def f(MW):\n\n        return (\n            MW**2\n            * (1.0 - MW**2 / MZ**2)\n            - target\n        )\n\n    return brentq(\n        f,\n        60.0,\n        90.0\n    )\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 13. RUN AUDIT\n# ================================================================\n\ndef run_audit():\n\n    print(\"=\" * 72)\n    print(\"PHI ELECTROWEAK TWO-LOOP THRESHOLD AUDIT\")\n    print(\"=\" * 72)\n\n    print()\n    print(\"INPUTS\")\n    print(\"-\" * 72)\n\n    print(f\"phi                  = {PHI:.15f}\")\n    print(f\"s_phi^2              = {S2_PHI:.12f}\")\n    print(f\"G_F                  = {GF:.12e}\")\n    print(f\"M_Z                  = {MZ:.6f} GeV\")\n    print(f\"M_t                  = {MT:.4f} GeV\")\n    print(f\"M_H                  = {MH:.4f} GeV\")\n    print(f\"alpha_s(MZ)           = {ALPHAS_MZ:.6f}\")\n    print(f\"MW experimental ref   = {MW_EXP:.6f} GeV\")\n\n    print()\n    print(\"TREE-LEVEL PHI TEST\")\n    print(\"-\" * 72)\n\n    print(\n        f\"MW(phi, tree)        = \"\n        f\"{MW_PHI_TREE:.6f} GeV\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"sW^2(OS, MW_exp)     = \"\n        f\"{S2_OS_EXP:.12f}\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"Delta r(exp MW)      = \"\n        f\"{DELTA_R_EXP:.8f}\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"Delta r(phi)         = \"\n        f\"{DELTA_R_PHI_TREE:.8f}\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"Delta r gap           = \"\n        f\"{DELTA_R_GAP:.8f}\"\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # 1-loop\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    print()\n    print(\"1-LOOP RG\")\n    print(\"-\" * 72)\n\n    sol1 = integrate_rg(two_loop=False)\n\n    roots1 = find_crossing(sol1)\n\n    if roots1:\n        mu1 = roots1[0]\n        s1 = s2_running(mu1, sol1)\n\n        print(f\"mu_phi^(1L)          = {mu1:.8f} GeV\")\n        print(f\"sW^2(mu_phi)         = {s1:.12f}\")\n    else:\n        mu1 = np.nan\n        print(\"No 1L crossing found.\")\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # 2-loop\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    print()\n    print(\"2-LOOP RG\")\n    print(\"-\" * 72)\n\n    sol2 = integrate_rg(two_loop=True)\n\n    roots2 = find_crossing(sol2)\n\n    if roots2:\n        mu2 = roots2[0]\n        s2 = s2_running(mu2, sol2)\n\n        print(f\"mu_phi^(2L)          = {mu2:.8f} GeV\")\n        print(f\"sW^2(mu_phi)         = {s2:.12f}\")\n    else:\n        mu2 = np.nan\n        print(\"No 2L crossing found.\")\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # matched\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    print()\n    print(\"2-LOOP + FINITE THRESHOLD DIAGNOSTIC\")\n    print(\"-\" * 72)\n\n    roots_match = find_matched_crossing(sol2)\n\n    if roots_match:\n        mu_match = roots_match[0]\n\n        s_match = s2_running(\n            mu_match,\n            sol2\n        )\n\n        threshold = finite_threshold_estimate(\n            mu_match\n        )\n\n        print(\n            f\"mu_phi^(2L+match)   = \"\n            f\"{mu_match:.8f} GeV\"\n        )\n\n        print(\n            f\"sW^2(running)       = \"\n            f\"{s_match:.12f}\"\n        )\n\n        print(\n            f\"delta_threshold     = \"\n            f\"{threshold:.12e}\"\n        )\n\n    else:\n        mu_match = np.nan\n        threshold = np.nan\n\n        print(\"No matched crossing found.\")\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Stability\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    if np.isfinite(mu1) and np.isfinite(mu2):\n\n        delta_mu = mu2 - mu1\n        rel_mu = delta_mu / mu1\n\n    else:\n\n        delta_mu = np.nan\n        rel_mu = np.nan\n\n    if np.isfinite(mu2) and np.isfinite(mu_match):\n\n        delta_mu_match = mu_match - mu2\n        rel_mu_match = delta_mu_match / mu2\n\n    else:\n\n        delta_mu_match = np.nan\n        rel_mu_match = np.nan\n\n    print()\n    print(\"SCALE STABILITY\")\n    print(\"-\" * 72)\n\n    print(\n        f\"1L -> 2L delta_mu    = \"\n        f\"{delta_mu:.8f} GeV\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"1L -> 2L relative    = \"\n        f\"{rel_mu:.6e}\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"2L -> matching       = \"\n        f\"{delta_mu_match:.8f} GeV\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"2L -> matching rel.  = \"\n        f\"{rel_mu_match:.6e}\"\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # MW tests\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    print()\n    print(\"M_W TEST\")\n    print(\"-\" * 72)\n\n    MW_1L_TREE = (\n        mw_from_s2_tree(S2_PHI)\n    )\n\n    MW_EXP_DIFF = (\n        MW_1L_TREE - MW_EXP\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"MW(phi tree)         = \"\n        f\"{MW_1L_TREE:.6f} GeV\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"MW(exp reference)    = \"\n        f\"{MW_EXP:.6f} GeV\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"delta MW             = \"\n        f\"{MW_EXP_DIFF:+.6f} GeV\"\n    )\n\n    print(\n        f\"delta MW / MW        = \"\n        f\"{MW_EXP_DIFF / MW_EXP:+.6e}\"\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Classification\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    # RG pass:\n    rg_pass = (\n        np.isfinite(mu2)\n        and abs(mu2 - MU_REFERENCE)\n        / MU_REFERENCE\n        < 0.05\n    )\n\n    # Threshold diagnostic:\n    threshold_reasonable = (\n        np.isfinite(threshold)\n        and abs(threshold) < 0.01\n    )\n\n    # Observable tree-level agreement:\n    mw_tree_pass = (\n        abs(MW_EXP_DIFF) < 0.01\n    )\n\n    if rg_pass and threshold_reasonable:\n        rg_status = \"PASS\"\n    elif np.isfinite(mu2):\n        rg_status = \"INCONCLUSIVE\"\n    else:\n        rg_status = \"FAIL\"\n\n    if mw_tree_pass:\n        mw_status = \"PASS\"\n    else:\n        mw_status = \"FAIL\"\n\n    print()\n    print(\"AUDIT CLASSIFICATION\")\n    print(\"-\" * 72)\n\n    print(f\"RG crossing           : {rg_status}\")\n    print(f\"MW tree-level         : {mw_status}\")\n\n    print()\n    print(\n        \"IMPORTANT: MW tree-level failure does NOT by itself \"\n        \"falsify the MSbar crossing.\"\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Summary dataframe\n    # \"mu_reference_GeV\":\n            MU_REFERENCE,\n\n        \"mu_phi_1L_GeV\":\n            mu1,\n\n        \"mu_phi_2L_GeV\":\n            mu2,\n\n        \"mu_phi_2L_match_GeV\":\n            mu_match,\n\n        \"delta_mu_1L_to_2L_GeV\":\n            delta_mu,\n\n        \"relative_shift_1L_to_2L\":\n            rel_mu,\n\n        \"delta_mu_2L_to_match_GeV\":\n            delta_mu_match,\n\n        \"relative_shift_2L_to_match\":\n            rel_mu_match,\n\n        \"delta_threshold\":\n            threshold,\n\n        \"MW_phi_tree_GeV\":\n            MW_PHI_TREE,\n\n        \"MW_exp_GeV\":\n            MW_EXP,\n\n        \"MW_difference_GeV\":\n            MW_EXP_DIFF,\n\n        \"MW_relative_difference\":\n            MW_EXP_DIFF / MW_EXP,\n\n        \"s2_OS_exp\":\n            S2_OS_EXP,\n\n        \"delta_r_exp\":\n            DELTA_R_EXP,\n\n        \"delta_r_phi\":\n            DELTA_R_PHI_TREE,\n\n        \"delta_r_gap\":\n            DELTA_R_GAP,\n\n        \"RG_status\":\n            rg_status,\n\n        \"MW_tree_status\":\n            mw_status\n    }])\n\n    summary.to_csv(\n        os.path.join(\n            RESULT_DIR,\n            \"phi_ew_audit_summary.csv\"\n        ),\n        index=False\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Running scan\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    mus = np.geomspace(\n        MU_MIN,\n        MU_MAX,\n        N_SCAN\n    )\n\n    rows = []\n\n    for mu in mus:\n\n        s1v = s2_running(mu, sol1)\n        s2v = s2_running(mu, sol2)\n        th = finite_threshold_estimate(mu)\n\n        rows.append({\n\n            \"mu_GeV\":\n                mu,\n\n            \"s2_1L\":\n                s1v,\n\n            \"s2_2L\":\n                s2v,\n\n            \"s_phi2\":\n                S2_PHI,\n\n            \"difference_1L\":\n                s1v - S2_PHI,\n\n            \"difference_2L\":\n                s2v - S2_PHI,\n\n            \"threshold\":\n                th,\n\n            \"matched_difference\":\n                s2v - S2_PHI - th\n        })\n\n    scan = pd.DataFrame(rows)\n\n    scan.to_csv(\n        os.path.join(\n            RESULT_DIR,\n            \"phi_ew_scan.csv\"\n        ),\n        index=False\n    )\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Plot\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))\n\n    plt.semilogx(\n        scan[\"mu_GeV\"],\n        scan[\"s2_1L\"],\n        label=\"1-loop RG\"\n    )\n\n    plt.semilogx(\n        scan[\"mu_GeV\"],\n        scan[\"s2_2L\"],\n        label=\"2-loop RG\"\n    )\n\n    plt.axhline(\n        S2_PHI,\n        linestyle=\"--\",\n        label=r\"$3/(5\\phi^2)$\"\n    )\n\n    plt.axvline(\n        MU_REFERENCE,\n        linestyle=\":\",\n        label=\"60.20 GeV plt.ylabel(r\"$s_W^2(\\mu)$\")\n\n    plt.title(\n        \"Electroweak Running and Golden-Ratio Projection\"\n    )\n\n    plt.legend()\n    plt.grid(True, alpha=0.3)\n\n    plt.tight_layout()\n\n {DELTA_R_EXP:.10f}\nDelta r(phi)            = {DELTA_R_PHI_TREE:.10f}\nDelta r gap             = {DELTA_R_GAP:.10f}\n\nRG CROSSING\n-----------\nmu_phi 1-loop           = {mu1}\nmu_phi 2-loop GeV\nrelative                = {rel_mu_match}\n\nTHRESHOLD\n---------\ndelta_threshold         = {threshold}\n\nCLASSIFICATION\n--------------\nRG                      = {rg_status}\nMW tree MSbar crossing near\n60.20 GeV survives:\n\n  1. two-loop RG evolution,\n  2. finite threshold matching,\n  3. MSbar -> on-shell conversion,\n  4. prediction of MW.\n\nThe finite threshold term in this script is a diagnostic\nparametrization and must be replaced by complete two-loop\nelectroweak matching coefficients for a publication-grade result.\n\n======================================================================\n\"\"\"\n\n    with open(\n        os.path.join(\n            RESULT_DIR,\n            \"phi_ew_report.txt\"\n        ),\n        \"w\"\n    ) as f:\n\n        f.write(report)\n\n    print()\n    print(\"=\" * 72)\n    print(\"FILES GENERATED\")\n    print(\"=\" * 72)\n\n    for filename in os.listdir(RESULT_DIR):\n\n        print(\n            os.path.join(\n                RESULT_DIR,\n                filename\n            )\n        )\n\n    print()\n    print(report)\n\n    return {\n        \"summary\": summary,\n        \"scan\": scan,\n        \"sensitivity\": sensitivity,\n        \"report\": report\n    }\n\n\n# ================================================================\n# 14.
